Physical · Atoms, moles & gases

Volumetric Analysis & Redox Titrations

Principle and indicators

\[\text{n-factor equivalents: }N_1V_1=N_2V_2\ (\text{at the end point})\]
TitrationIndicatorEnd point
Strong acid – strong baseAny (methyl orange, phenolphthalein)pH 7
Weak acid – strong basePhenolphthalein (8.3–10)pH > 7
Strong acid – weak baseMethyl orange (3.1–4.4)pH < 7
\(\mathrm{KMnO_4}\) (acidic)Self-indicator (pink)Permanent pale pink
Iodometry (\(\mathrm{I_2/S_2O_3^{2-}}\))Starch (blue → colourless)Disappearance of blue

Redox titrations and equivalent weights

  • \(\mathrm{MnO_4^-+8H^++5e^-\to Mn^{2+}+4H_2O}\): \(E=M/5\) (neutral: \(M/3\); strongly alkaline: \(M/1\)). Never acidify with HCl (Cl⁻ oxidised) or \(\mathrm{HNO_3}\); use dil. \(\mathrm{H_2SO_4}\).
  • \(\mathrm{Cr_2O_7^{2-}+14H^++6e^-\to2Cr^{3+}+7H_2O}\): \(E=M/6\) (needs an external/redox indicator, e.g. diphenylamine).
  • Oxalic acid with \(\mathrm{KMnO_4}\) (heated to about 330 K): \(\mathrm{2MnO_4^-+5C_2O_4^{2-}+16H^+\to2Mn^{2+}+10CO_2+8H_2O}\), \(E=M/2\). Mohr’s salt \(\mathrm{FeSO_4(NH_4)_2SO_4\cdot6H_2O}\) (M = 392): \(\mathrm{Fe^{2+}\to Fe^{3+}}\), \(E=M\).
  • Iodometry: \(\mathrm{2Cu^{2+}+4I^-\to Cu_2I_2+I_2}\); \(\mathrm{I_2+2S_2O_3^{2-}\to2I^-+S_4O_6^{2-}}\). Equivalence: \(\mathrm{Cu^{2+}}\) \(E=M\); \(\mathrm{Cr_2O_7^{2-}}\) \(E=M/6\); \(\mathrm{H_2O_2}\) \(E=17\).
  • Back titration: excess reagent added, unreacted part titrated (used for insoluble \(\mathrm{CaCO_3}\), \(\mathrm{NH_3}\) estimation). Double titration: \(\mathrm{Na_2CO_3+NaHCO_3}\) using phenolphthalein (\(V_1\): \(\mathrm{CO_3^{2-}\to HCO_3^-}\)) then methyl orange (\(V_2\)): \(\mathrm{Na_2CO_3}\propto V_1\), \(\mathrm{NaHCO_3}\propto V_2-V_1\) (in terms of volumes of same acid).
  • Limiting reagent: divide moles by the stoichiometric coefficient; the smallest value is limiting. % yield \(=\dfrac{\text{actual}}{\text{theoretical}}\times100\).
39
Inorganic · Elements & compounds

Hydrogen, Water & Hydrogen Peroxide

Hydrogen and hydrides

  • Isotopes: protium, deuterium (D), tritium (T, radioactive, \(t_{1/2}=12.3\) y). Laboratory: \(\mathrm{Zn+H_2SO_4}\); industrial: steam reforming \(\mathrm{CH_4+H_2O\xrightarrow{Ni,1270K}CO+3H_2}\), water-gas shift \(\mathrm{CO+H_2O\to CO_2+H_2}\), electrolysis of water.
  • Hydrides: ionic/saline (s-block, \(\mathrm{NaH,CaH_2}\)); covalent/molecular (p-block; electron-deficient \(\mathrm{B_2H_6}\), electron-precise \(\mathrm{CH_4}\), electron-rich \(\mathrm{NH_3,H_2O}\)); metallic/interstitial (d-block, \(\mathrm{PdH_{0.6}}\)).
  • Ortho- and para-hydrogen (nuclear-spin isomers); atomic hydrogen torch gives about 4000 K.

Hardness of water

  • Temporary: \(\mathrm{Ca(HCO_3)_2,Mg(HCO_3)_2}\); removed by boiling or Clark’s method (\(\mathrm{Ca(OH)_2}\)). Permanent: chlorides and sulphates of Ca, Mg; removed by washing soda, Calgon \(\mathrm{Na_6P_6O_{18}}\) (forms soluble \(\mathrm{[Na_2CaP_6O_{18}]^{2-}}\)), zeolite (permutit) or ion-exchange resins.
  • Hardness (ppm) \(=\dfrac{\text{mass of }\mathrm{CaCO_3}}{\text{mass of water}}\times10^6\).

Heavy water (D₂O)

  • Prepared by prolonged electrolysis of water or fractional distillation; \(\mathrm{b.p.}\ 374.4\ \mathrm K\), m.p. 276.8 K, maximum density at 11.6 °C. Used as moderator in nuclear reactors and as tracer; reactions: \(\mathrm{Al_4C_3+12D_2O\to4Al(OD)_3+3CD_4}\); \(\mathrm{Mg_3N_2+6D_2O\to3Mg(OD)_2+2ND_3}\).

Hydrogen peroxide

  • Preparation: \(\mathrm{BaO_2+H_2SO_4\to BaSO_4+H_2O_2}\); industrial: auto-oxidation of 2-ethylanthraquinol. Storage in dark/plastic bottles with urea or glycerol (stabilisers).
  • Structure: non-planar, open-book; O–O–H angle 94.8° (gas), dihedral angle 111.5° (gas), 90.2° (solid).
  • Oxidising agent (acidic: \(\mathrm{H_2O_2+2H^++2e^-\to2H_2O}\); basic \(\mathrm{HO_2^-+H_2O+2e^-\to3OH^-}\)): \(\mathrm{2Fe^{2+}+H_2O_2+2H^+\to2Fe^{3+}+2H_2O}\); \(\mathrm{PbS+4H_2O_2\to PbSO_4+4H_2O}\); \(\mathrm{2KI\to I_2}\). Reducing agent: \(\mathrm{2MnO_4^-+5H_2O_2+6H^+\to2Mn^{2+}+5O_2+8H_2O}\); \(\mathrm{Ag_2O+H_2O_2\to2Ag+O_2+H_2O}\); \(\mathrm{HOCl}\), \(\mathrm{O_3}\), \(\mathrm{Cl_2}\) reduced to Cl⁻.
  • Volume strength relations: see Stoichiometry (\(M=V/11.2\)). Uses: bleaching, antiseptic, rocket propellant.
